🔴 **Legacy support gap:** legacy S2S supports the feature (fully or within stated limits), while v0.2-my-try does not. Intentional exclusions are marked too; unverified legacy support is not.

Doc Status: supporting

The three example columns display **87 concrete PHP++ probes** and their actual
**S2S generated v1** (legacy `generators/php/`, strict profile) and **S2S generated v2**
(`compiler/my-try/`) output or diagnostics directly in the table. Complete emitted
bundles are shown using native Markdown code spans and minimal escaping. For preserved
line breaks, indentation, wider code columns and a sticky header, open the
[formatted HTML table](status_comparison_2026_10_08.html). References remain
next to the inline code for provenance and separate viewing. One artifact-layout row reuses the
multi-file probe; three compiler/build protocol rows are explicitly N/A.

**Emitted C++ is not a native compilation or execution pass.** Legacy is type-blind
and sometimes emits placeholders, missing composition, or unchecked calls. **⚠**
flags an observed limitation or a proof boundary needing special care. Generation
errors include the exact saved diagnostic; they may expose only the first blocker.
Samples cover representative subsets, not every feature grouped into a row. Existing
availability assessments retain their original broader evidence and are not silently
promoted by emission alone. The [sample index and collection details](status_samples_2026_10_08/README.md)
explain the results, source compatibility differences, and reproducible commands.
No native compiler build, emitted-program execution, STAN run or tests/php suite run
was performed. All source/output/log files are isolated in this chat's new evidence folder.

The **Required in v0.2 compiler** column audits the converted strict PHP++ compiler
project, not features merely described in its generated-code strings. See the
[source evidence and definitions](compiler_phpp_requirements_2026_10_08.md) and
[204-file snapshot manifest](compiler_phpp_requirements_manifest_2026_10_08.json).
**Yes — compiler/framework** means required to compile that source unchanged;
**No use found** is not a language exclusion. **Behavior** and **Workflow** separate
compiler responsibilities from source-language requirements. Compatibility rows
may be resolved by adapting the converter/source rather than expanding v0.2 semantics.
Typed Storage collections are recorded as a **public requirement for all programs**,
per user direction; their current optional-module packaging is not universal availability.

Changes in this source-requirement refresh:

- Added the fourth column to all 72 prior feature rows and added 19 source-specific rows.
- Exposed Storage/cursor/Iterator integration, object-keyed hashes, exact container
  constructors, object identity, readonly fields, converter aliases/global lookup,
  error inspection, and required runtime API groups.
- Confirmed closures with value captures, throw/catch/finally and covariant returns
  are compiler-source requirements. Reference foreach, reference captures, arrow
  functions, traits and boxed JSON are not used by the inspected compiler source.
- Corrected take availability: my-try accepts both helper and assignment spellings.
- Preserved legacy test conclusions. Added bindings/implementation evidence is
  explicitly labeled; it does not imply additional green tests or self-compilation.

R01–R16 below refer to sections of the source evidence document linked from every
requirement cell. New runtime support assessments concern APIs exposed to compiled
programs, not facilities already used by the PHP-hosted compiler implementation.

Legacy column refreshed from [the test expectation ledger](legacy_test_expectations_2026_10_08.md).
309 metadata files reviewed, excluding all of `tests/php/operators/`: 306 active,
three disabled. The active expectations comprise 245 execution cases, 35 compile-only
cases, 25 generation rejections and one compilation rejection. All active tests are
presumed green per user instruction; none were run. That earlier test-only refresh
left my-try statuses unchanged except for separating vector removal. The subsequent
source-requirement refresh adds the narrower compatibility/API rows described above.

A **tests:** link marks evidence from this refresh. **Prior assessment only** preserves
an earlier documentation/source conclusion where this restricted test review supplies
no matching active expectation; it is not a new support or rejection finding. Red markers
still include those prior-assessment gaps and do not claim every red row has test evidence.
Negative filenames are ignored in favor of active expectation fields. Disabled placeholder
success expectations and historical reference-matrix fixtures are not counted as support.

Changes in the earlier legacy-test refresh:

- Late static binding/shared factory basics: **Unverified → Yes, bounded**, from
  classes 022–026. These do not establish instance late binding or arbitrary factory forms.
- Reference foreach: **Partial/provisional description → Yes for tested element updates**,
  including vector, hash, mixed tables and wrapper vectors. Structural-mutation safety
  remains unverified. Existing outer foreach value bindings are also explicitly tested.
- Added a red **vector-element unset** row: int/string removal and compaction are positive
  legacy expectations, while the existing my-try CH06 assessment defers this operation.
- Capture support now has direct positive expectations (value snapshots and reference
  observation), rather than relying on conflicting legacy documentation.
- Namespace alias import is an explicit **expected compilation failure**, while class,
  function and constant imports have positive expectations.
- Broader grouped claims are narrowed to actual test scope: e.g. terminating recursion,
  unit enums, struct/visibility breadth and explicit array-cast/nullable-guard behavior
  are not established by the matching basic cases.
- Arrow functions, throw/catch/finally, traits and several ownership/build capabilities
  have no matching active metadata evidence in this restricted scope; their earlier
  assessments remain clearly labeled, not silently promoted to test-backed conclusions.
- Three static/call/expression interpolation cases are disabled despite placeholder
  success expectations. Basic/braced/property/vector-index interpolation is active.


Snapshot: 2026-10-08. Checkout HEAD observed during review:

review while another agent is performing native validation, not a frozen-tree test run.

The table compares the strict PHS frontend → shared preparation → C++ path in
my-try against the existing `generators/php/` pipeline currently invoked by `scpp`.
“Legacy S2S” names that pipeline, not exclusively its legacy PHP profile. Older
annotations and strict spellings can represent the same capability; exact source
compatibility is not implied. LLVM execution and a complete builtin/library inventory remain outside scope;
runtime APIs actually used by this converted compiler are now included.

**Yes** means an implemented capability with supporting repository evidence in the
stated subset. **Partial** calls out a material restriction. **No** means an explicit
unsupported/deferred decision or missing integration. **Unverified** means this review
did not establish availability; it is neither a rejection nor a promise. For the refreshed
legacy column, active test expectations are presumed green by user
instruction. Documentation-only claims remain separately labeled. No test suites or native compiler
builds were run. The later example collection invoked both S2S generators only.

CH01–CH11 completion is bounded completion, not implementation of every imported
example. CH12/CH13 remain major legacy capability gaps. CH14 is pending as a chapter,
but it contains concepts already implemented under other owners. CH15 is a mixed
register of missing features, design decisions and maintenance; it is not a list of
features that all previously worked. The selected CH15 rows include reference iteration
and advisory delivery, where legacy evidence exists, plus explicit negative comparisons
to avoid treating traits, reflection and dynamic properties as regressions.

The documented native compiler checkpoint remains CH10 (2026-10-07), including
426 dedicated class/struct native/PHP artifact comparisons and 428 generated-program
executions. CH11 records PHP-host and generated-C++ proofs, including the selected
variance/cross-cast/destructor work, with native compiler validation outstanding in
the inspected handoff. The other agent's ongoing build is not claimed as completed.
Compiling a generated program and rebuilding the compiler itself are separate proofs.
See the [native evidence owner](portability/conversion_review.md).
